       P ROOF : The triangle inequality gives f ( x ) ≤ zv + L∥ x − v∥1 . Choose an attaining
18                                                                                                     18
     original site xi . In each coordinate choose the corner coordinate lying between x j and
19                                                                                                     19
     xi,j . The resulting v ∈ V (C ) satisfies ∥ x − xi ∥1 = ∥ x − v∥1 + ∥v − xi ∥1 , proving (49).
20                                                                                                     20
     Choose now i = i∗ ( x ). At this corner an owner with a strictly smaller transformed
21                                                                                                     21
     value would improve the objective at x; an owner with the same value and a
22                                                                                                     22
     smaller original index would contradict the prescribed tie rule. Hence ov = i∗ ( x ) for
23                                                                                                     23
     a suitable corner. Every candidate in (50) is an original cone, so its lexicographic
24                                                                                                     24
     minimum is the desired pair. Coordinatewise forward and backward minimizations
25                                                                                                     25
     evaluate the separable ℓ1 transform; keeping original indices in each lexicographic
26                                                                                                     26
     comparison preserves ties. The supplement gives the line invariant, operation
27                                                                                                     27
     account and numerical enclosure details.                                                 Q.E.D.
28                                                                                                     28

29     The nodal distance transform is classical; the linear-time coordinate algorithm is              29

30   described by Felzenszwalb and Huttenlocher (2012). We use it for a different com- 30
31   putational obligation: continuous off-grid Bellman queries must retain the original               31

32   feasible action and the specified tie rule. Equation (49) alone does not authorize                32
     42

1    using the action attached to v; it is (50) that licenses the policy. The result belongs       1

2    to the cone-based NBO construction, but an identical native min-plus implemen- 2
3    tation can use the same compiler. It does not establish a computational property              3

4    exclusive to a neural encoding. Proposition 13 remains an equality statement, not a           4

5    comparison between independently trained methods.                                             5

10                                                                                                 10

     Primitive evaluation, feasible-net construction, integration, error accounting, actor
16                                                                                                 16
     formation, repair and output are additional terms. Exact arithmetic has a bit cost;

       In the two-state investment study, S = ( N + 1)2 , J = N + 1 and M = N. At                  20

16     P ROOF : The first two allocations make the right side of (54) at most ε/2. The                16

17   acquisition and quantization terms add at most another ε/2 by (59). Compilation                  17

18   preserves the selected policy, so it does not change this account. On the dyadic                 18

19   ladder, the sum of every positive power of the earlier resolutions is bounded by                 19

20   a constant times that power of its largest resolution. Summing the complete alge- 20
21   braic operation counts and retained arrays proves the resource assertions. Effective             21

22   certified queries at the prescribed accuracy, rather than unchecked fixed-precision              22

23   evaluations, are part of the hypotheses.                                              Q.E.D.     23

24                                                                                                    24

25
       The corollary turns the compiler into a sufficient work-to-economic-accuracy                   25

26
     allocation. A finite experimental ladder can still end before the sufficient resolution;         26

27
     its failure must remain a failure. Nor does more precise observation eliminate                   27

28
     construction error: the two portions of the budget are distinct.                                 28

29                                                                                                    29
                      11.2. Certified numerical queries and selector ambiguity
30                                                                                                    30

31     The executable compiler computes (zv , ov ) in rational arithmetic. A value query              31

32   encloses each corner expression outward and takes the minima of lower and upper                  32
     44

1    endpoints. For a state box contained in one cell, the possible original actors are      1

2    among its corner owners; for a box crossing cells, the union of their corner owners     2

3    is used. An owner is discarded only when its lower objective enclosure exceeds          3

4    another candidate’s upper enclosure. Exact ambiguous point queries use rational         4

5    comparisons and the original lexicographic rule. Uncertain-state queries retain         5

6    the hull of all remaining actions and propagate that hull through repair and the        6

7    dynamics.                                                                               7

8      Thus a floating-point tie is not silently assigned to a convenient policy. A numeri- 8
9    cal implementation that deliberately returns a nonattaining index instead has to pay    9

10   the νt allowance in (42). The present frozen-object checks separate exact identities    10

11   from outward-rounding diagnostics. Overlapping machine intervals are useful             11

12   checks; the identity theorem, rather than that overlap, proves that the compiler        12

13   represents the original policy.                                                         13

14                                                                                           14

15               12. AN ORACLE-EXPLICIT CONSTRAINED INVESTMENT ECONOMY                       15

16                                                                                           16
       The following evidence is the frozen R47 study. Its results and limitations are
17                                                                                           17
     retained; Section 15 gives the new execution and decision evidence. We now execute
18                                                                                           18
     the feasible-witness construction in a coupled two-state economy. Capital affects
19                                                                                           19
     both future production conditions and current investment capacity. This makes fea-
20                                                                                           20
     sible repair part of the economic policy rather than an algebraic example appended
21                                                                                           21
     to a common-action calculation.
22                                                                                           22
       Let x ∈ [0, 1]2 , β = 15/16, and 0 ≤ a ≤ κ ( x ) = 1/8 + ( x1 + x2 )/16. A common
23                                                                                           23
     innovation z is continuously uniform on [−1/32, 1/32], with opposite exposures in
24                                                                                           24
     the two transitions:
25                                                                                           25

26                                                                                           26
                 F1 ( x, a, z) = 1/16 + x1 /2 + x2 /8 + x1 (1 − x2 )/16 + a/2 + z,    (52)
27                                                                                           27

28               F2 ( x, a, z) = 1/16 + x2 /2 + x1 /8 + x2 (1 − x1 )/16 + a/4 − z.           28

29                                                                                           29

30   Define the shortage and imbalance cost                                                  30

31                                                                                           31

32                          b( x ) = ( x1 − x2 )2 /4 + 2(1/2 − x1 − x2 )2+ .                 32
                                   NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                   45

1    Stage cost is ∑2j=1 ( x j − 5/8)2 + b( x ) + pa2 + 4a4 ; terminal cost doubles the squared- 1
2    target terms and retains b. The predeclared cells have T ∈ {2, 3} and p ∈ {1, 4}. 2
3    These are normalized theoretical economies, not estimated parameters or calibrated             3

4    welfare magnitudes.                                                                            4

5                                                                                                   5

6      P ROPOSITION 16—Finite primitives and continuous-law queries: The state domain               6

7    is invariant under (52). In the ℓ1 state norm, valid primitive constants are                   7

8                                                                                                   8
     Cx = 13/4,     L g = 9/2,   Fx = 11/16,     Fa = 3/4,    Ca = p/2 + 1/4,       K A = 1/16.

     The uniform tensor state grid with N subdivisions has covering radius h = 1/N. At each
11                                                                                                  11
     node, N + 1 equally spaced feasible actions have covering radius at most k = 1/(8N ). The
12                                                                                                  12
     repair is min{ ai , κ ( x )} and has zero extra displacement allowance. If a continuation is
13                                                                                                  13
     L-Lipschitz, M uniform midpoint cells enclose its original continuous-law expectation after
14                                                                                                  14
     adding the deterministic remainder L/(32M) to the numerical integration error.
15                                                                                                  15

16                                                                                                  16
       P ROOF : The primitive formulas give F1 ∈ [1/32, 29/32] and F2 ∈ [1/32, 27/32]
17                                                                                                  17
     by interval bounds. The sums of absolute derivatives in each state column are at
18
     most 11/16, and the action derivative has ℓ1 norm 3/4. Differentiating the costs               18

19                                                                                                  19
     on the two shortage regions gives (53). Capacity is 1/16-Lipschitz and at most
20                                                                                                  20
     1/4, proving the grid and repair assertions. Within a uniform shock cell, the mean
21                                                                                                  21
     distance to its midpoint is 1/(64M). Since the transition has shock modulus two in
22
     ℓ1 , the expectation error is at most L/(32M). Thus quadrature does not change the             22

23                                                                                                  23
     innovation law to a discrete one.                                                   Q.E.D.
24                                                                                                  24

25     The two generators are the feasible cone witness and conventional fitted-value               25

26   iteration in the tensor bilinear basis. Each works backward against its own fitted             26

27   future, uses the same feasible action net, and evaluates the same continuous law               27

28   with the preceding remainder. No optimal value or other method’s continuation is               28

29   supplied as a label. Rounded labels, their valid query errors, slopes, node actions            29

30   and all policy bounds are stored.                                                              30

31     The conventional actor selects a nearest state node and repairs its action at                31

32   the current capacity. It receives the one-sided account rather than only the older             32
     46

1    separated certificate. For the bilinear interpolant, the Bellman-residual enclosure is          1

     proves the stated extension from the nodal enclosure.

                            12.1. A cap obtained from economic accuracy
25                                                                                                   25
       In this class the previously abstract covering, integration and repair operations
26                                                                                                   26
     are finite routines with explicit costs. Let ST = ∑t<T βt , a p = Cx + K A Ca and b =
27                                                                                                   27
     β( Fx + K A Fa ) = 705/1024. Define
28                                                                                                   28

29                                                                                                   29
                          L = max{ L g , a p /(1 − b)},      D = Ca + βFa L.
30                                                                                                   30

31   The primitive recursion gives Lt ≤ L and DtQ ≤ D at every horizon. Suppose that, 31
32   apart from the quadrature remainder, each nonterminal numerical query has error                 32
                                 NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                 47

1    at most ξ and terminal labels have error at most ξ T . With M = N, Theorem 10              1

2    implies                                                                                    2

3                                                                                               3

4                           G N ≤ CT,p /N + 2ST ξ + 2β T ξ T ,                           (54)   4

5
                           CT,p = ST { D/8 + 2L + βL/16} + 2β T L g .                           5

6                                                                                               6

7    Thus a dyadic N ≥ 2CT,p /ε and ξ, ξ T ≤ ε/[4(ST + β T )] suffice for loss at most ε. 7
8    Acquisition and action quantization add the explicit terms of (45); these must             8

9    receive part of the same tolerance. Fixed binary64, a fixed label lattice and a finite     9

10   three-rung study are not asserted to achieve every positive ε.                             10

11     For this two-state, one-action, one-shock implementation, Bellman queries num- 11
12   ber T ( N + 1)3 . Each has N midpoint evaluations. Direct cone evaluation examines         12

13   ( N + 1)2 cones, giving O( TN 6 ) primitive arithmetic work. Bilinear future evalua- 13
14   tion uses a local patch, giving O( TN 4 ) work for that implementation. Both store         14

15   O( TN 2 ) critic labels and actions. The present batched program additionally holds        15

16   O( N 3 ) state–action arrays and O( BN 2 ) temporary cone entries for fixed batch size     16

17   B. Feasible acquisition and repair add their recorded deployment work, rather than         17

18   being included at zero cost.                                                               18

19     With pb -bit arithmetic and multiplication cost M( pb ), the corresponding arith- 19
20   metic upper bounds acquire a factor M( pb + T + log N ), with coefficient conversion,      20

21   comparison and serialization charged separately. The precision must also meet              21

22   the query enclosure requirement. These are algorithmic upper bounds, not lower             22

23   bounds for all conventional methods. Proposition 13 shows that the identical native        23

24   envelope has the same leading factors as its ReLU realization. No representation           24

25   label removes the tensor dependence.                                                       25

30   economic cell. The 24 services store all 72 rungs, including failed attempts. Targets      30

31   are 4, 2, 1, 1/2 in the normalized cost units. They test the construction-to-certificate   31

32   service; a mathematically valid coarse bound is not automatically an economically          32

20   economic cell, but bilinear fitted-value iteration is faster in all twelve common suc- 20
21   cessful comparisons. These coarse all-state certificates establish an executed feasible                21

22   construction, not an economically precise solution at the unachieved tolerances. The                   22

24   requires without relabeling these failures.                                                            24

                          13. POLICY COSTS AND ECONOMIC METHOD CHOICE
18                                                                                                            18

19     The following evidence is the frozen R47 study. Its results and limitations are                        19

20   retained; Section 14 gives the new execution and decision evidence. We next evaluate                     20

21   the actual witness policy, not its distance bound relative to an unknown optimum. 21
22   The policies are the frozen objects at every common first crossing in the original                       22

23   witness catalogue. There are ten such economic-cell and target combinations. Three                       23

24   timing repetitions of an identical checkpoint are not three independent policies.                        24

25     The primary initial-state law is uniform on [0, 1]. The additional fixed states                        25

26   1/8, 1/2 and 7/8 test particular economic starting positions. At each of these                           26

27   forty groups, the three pairwise contrasts concern witness, nearest-cone and spline                      27

28   policies. A fixed design specifies 131,072 common paths, forty-bit innovation bins                       28

29   and family error 0.01 for all 120 contrasts. The economic comparison uses the same                       29

30   continuous innovations as the original economy.                                                          30

31     Let [zm , zm ] enclose g − f Tm and let the initial continuation difference have range                 31

32   [ a0 , b0 ] over the chosen initial-state law. With the one-sided residuals of (30), the                 32

     For a common random initial state, one bounds the difference f 0m − f 0n , not the two
8                                                                                                     8

9                                                                                                     9

10                                                                                                    10
     law extend Theorem 4 to this estimand.
11                                                                                                    11
       Every simulated bin generates lower and upper score endpoints. When a state
12                                                                                                    12
     enclosure meets a selector boundary, the calculation encloses all possible actors and
13                                                                                                    13
     subsequent states. It neither drops the path nor substitutes an unverified midpoint
14                                                                                                    14

          T HEOREM 18—Simultaneous policy comparison and adoption: Conditional on the
21                                                                                                    21
     frozen policies and the independent uniform-bin model, the endpoint intervals constructed
22                                                                                                    22
     with (55) and (15) cover all declared expected policy-cost differences simultaneously with
23                                                                                                    23
     probability at least 0.99. If an interval [ Lmn , Umn ] has Umn < 0, policy m has smaller
24                                                                                                    24
     expected cost than n on this event. For a previously specified tolerance η, Umn ≤ η certifies
25                                                                                                    25
     noninferiority within that tolerance, whereas [ Lmn , Umn ] ⊂ [−η, η ] certifies two-sided
26                                                                                                    26
     proximity.
27                                                                                                    27
       More generally, fix a reference policy b and known implementation charges k m . Suppose
28                                                                                                    28
     simultaneous intervals [ Lm , Um ] enclose Jm − Jb for a finite candidate catalogue, including
29                                                                                                    29
     [0, 0] for b. Select m
                          b to minimize Um + k m . On the coverage event,
30                                                                                                    30

31                                                                                                    31

32                           Jmb + k mb ≤ min{ Jm + k m + (Um − Lm )}.                        (56)    32
                                              m
                                       NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                        51

1    Thus an economic choice made after seeing all intervals retains its declared uncertainty               1

2    account. The catalogue comparison is within one common economic task and initial-state                 2

3    law, not across different prices, horizons or evaluations.                                             3

4                                                                                                           4
       P ROOF : The score identity identifies the expected difference. Its interval end-
5                                                                                                           5
     points are bounded independent observations within each group. Apply the one-
6                                                                                                           6
     sided empirical Bernstein inequality of Maurer and Pontil (2009) to each lower
7                                                                                                           7
     and upper endpoint and take a union bound over 240 tails. The numerical enclo-
8                                                                                                           8
     sures only enlarge the valid intervals. Independence between different contrasts is
9                                                                                                           9
     unnecessary. On the resulting event, Jmb − Jb ≤ Umb and Um ≤ Jm − Jb + (Um − Lm ).
10                                                                                                          10
     Minimize the former upper bound plus the known charge to obtain (56).                        Q.E.D.
11                                                                                                          11

12     The study fixes η = 1/1024 in its normalized cost units before execution. It is                      12

13   a stated decision tolerance, not an estimated welfare threshold. Zero remains the                      13

14   superiority threshold. An interval merely intersecting zero meets neither criterion                    14

15   automatically. The fixed pseudorandom implementation supplies repeatable inputs;                       15

16   it does not prove the independent sampling model. The theorem states that model                        16

17   explicitly. No claim about initialization risk or the distribution of newly trained                    17

18   policies follows from these frozen-policy comparisons.                                                 18

5                                                                                             5
                      13.2. Economic interpretation and the NBO program
6                                                                                             6
       The new evidence connects a feasible implementation to two distinct economic
7                                                                                             7
     statements. First, the acquisition-aware theorem bounds the loss of a policy that can
8                                                                                             8
     actually be executed under uncertain state measurement and capacity constraints.
9                                                                                             9
     Second, the direct experiment certifies proximity of the original scalar policy costs
10                                                                                            10
     within an explicitly chosen decision tolerance, so that a user of that catalogue may
11                                                                                            11
     consider implementation charges without treating separate regret bounds as values.
12                                                                                            12
     These statements are stronger than a certificate comparison alone.
13                                                                                            13
       Neither statement implies that a neural representation is indispensable. The iden-
14                                                                                            14
     tical native envelope is a mathematical control, and the conventional fitted-value
15                                                                                            15
     and spline implementations remain strong alternatives. The constrained experiment
16                                                                                            16
     demonstrates two-state continuous-uncertainty construction and nontrivial repair,
17                                                                                            17
     not a high-dimensional scaling result or an estimated economic application. Its tight
18                                                                                            18
     targets remain unachieved. The direct cost experiment does not cover the new con-
19                                                                                            19
     strained policies or nonlinear recursive utility; its expectation-specific conclusion
20                                                                                            20
     is not transferred to those settings. The controlled-diffusion, recursive-preference,
21                                                                                            21
     endogenous-preference, temporal-self and game developments below retain their
22                                                                                            22
     own conditions and original role in the NBO program. Their preservation is not
23                                                                                            23
     counted as a new comparative execution.
24                                                                                            24

25                                                                                            25
            14. PRICED OBSERVATION AND DIRECT CONSTRAINED-POLICY DECISIONS
26                                                                                            26

27     A state-error radius is an economic input when acquiring more precise infor- 27
28   mation is costly. We specify a finite-bit observation technology and a controller        28

29   replacement charge. These are primitives of a theoretical decision problem in re- 29
30   source units, not estimates from a calibrated economy. They permit a decision            30

31   interpretation without treating an interval containing zero as equality or treating an   31

32   all-state regret bound as a ranking of two policies.                                     32
     54

1                             14.1. Choosing the observation precision                              1

2                                                                                                   2
       On [0, 1]d , a b-bit sensor reports, in each coordinate, the containing dyadic interval
3                                                                                                   3
     and its midpoint. The right endpoint 1 belongs to the last interval. The resulting
4                                                                                                   4
     acquisition set has ℓ1 radius
5                                                                                                   5

6
                                          r (b) = d 2−(b+1) .                                (58)   6

7                                                                                                   7

8    For At ( x ) = [0, κt ( x )], use the lowest capacity on the reported box, then round the      8

9    selected action downward to spacing ∆t . Feasibility holds throughout that box. In             9

10   the affine capacity economy this lower capacity is computed exactly from the lower             10

11   box coordinates. The acquisition repair therefore has χt = 0 in Corollary 12.                  11

12     Suppose the same b is chosen before date zero and each coordinate-bit costs λ > 0            12

13   per date. The observation fee is paid in the same additive resource units as the               13

14   objective. With Bt = ∏s<t β s , it is Cb, where C = λd ∑t<T Bt . Write G for the full- 14
15   information witness loss certificate with exact selector and repair. The acquisition           15

16   theorem implies an augmented loss bound, relative to the full-information optimum              16

17   without the observation fee,                                                                   17

28        P ROPOSITION 19—Optimal precision for the certified economic account: On a                28

29   declared integer range bmin ≤ b ≤ bmax , the smallest minimizer of the right side of (59) is   29

30   characterized by the neighboring inequalities                                                  30

1    with the smallest minimizer selected when equality gives two. The criterion can be decided       1

2    by rational comparisons, without an unverified logarithm. It minimizes a proved upper            2

3    account; it need not minimize the unknown actual policy cost as a function of precision.         3

4                                                                                                     4

     sary and sufficient, with the indicated endpoint convention. Equation (59) follows               7
                                acq

       This calculation provides an explicit information-price comparative static: an
10                                                                                                    10
     increase in λ weakly decreases the smallest minimizing precision, while an increase
11                                                                                                    11
     in the sensitivity account A weakly increases it. The result concerns the guarantee,
12                                                                                                    12
     not a fitted distribution of measurement errors. The executed curves use every
13                                                                                                    13
     b = 4, . . . , 16 and every λ ∈ {1/16384, 1/4096, 1/1024}. All curves and endpoint

                                 14.2. The actual constrained-policy cost
17                                                                                                    17

18     Fix the two-state witness and bilinear-FVI policies stored at the finest original              18

19   R47 resolution, N = 16. In each economic cell, the two policies are evaluated with               19

20   the same observation contract: exact state, six bits per coordinate, or ten bits per             20

21   coordinate. The finite-bit policies use robust capacity repair and action spacing                21

22   1/4096; the exact-state policies are unquantized. Thus there are three distinct pairs            22

23   of implemented policies, not three observations of an assumed continuous actor.                  23

24     For an initial law µ, the estimand is                                                          24

          T HEOREM 20—Interval simulation and replacement decisions: For each of K fixed
9                                                                                                        9
     policy-pair tasks, partition the underlying initial and innovation uniforms into equal bins
10                                                                                                       10
     and draw independent uniform bin indices. Suppose a numerical path routine returns
11                                                                                                       11

12                                                                                                       12

13                                                                                                       13

14                                                                                                       14
     m L , mU enclose their sample means and s2L , sU

     If replacing either installed controller costs k > 0 once at date zero, an interval strictly
24                                                                                                       24
     inside (−k, k ) proves that neither replacement recoups that fee in expected resource cost for
25                                                                                                       25
     that task. An interval with upper endpoint below zero ranks the witness policy below FVI; a
26                                                                                                       26
     lower endpoint above zero ranks it above FVI. Containing zero does not establish equality.
27                                                                                                       27

28        P ROOF : Conditional on a bin sequence, integrating the continuous uniform                     28

29   variables gives a conditional path expectation between its enclosing endpoints. 29
30   Averaging over uniform bin indices reproduces the original continuous law, so                       30

31   EL j ≤ Dµ,b ≤ EUj . Apply the empirical-Bernstein inequality of Maurer and Pontil                   31

32   (2009) to the lower and upper endpoint samples, with a union bound across 2K                        32
                                   NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                               57

1    one-sided assertions; certified moment and arithmetic allowances enlarge, rather           1

2    than shrink, the intervals. Finally, switching from FVI to witness changes cost by         2

3    Dµ,b + k, while switching in the opposite direction changes it by k − Dµ,b . Both are      3

4    positive when | Dµ,b | < k.                                                     Q.E.D.     4

5                                                                                               5
       The experiment fixes K = 48, n = 262144 and α = 0.01 before execution. An exact
6                                                                                               6
     rational exponential bound verifies that ℓ = 10 is sufficient. Forty-bit bins cover the
7                                                                                               7
     continuous initial and innovation laws. The initial laws are independent uniform co-
8                                                                                               8
     ordinates and the three fixed vectors (1/8, 1/8), (1/2, 1/2) and (7/8, 7/8), crossed
9                                                                                               9
     with all four horizon–price cells and three sensors. A fixed pseudorandom stream
10                                                                                              10
     makes the records reproducible; it is not a proof that those indices are independent
11                                                                                              11
     random variables.
12                                                                                              12
       The replacement fee is k = 1/64, one quarter of the maximal quadratic investment
13                                                                                              13
     expenditure at p = 1. It is an explicit theoretical transaction charge. We do not
14                                                                                              14
     calibrate it to welfare data, choose it after inspecting the intervals, or infer an all-
15                                                                                              15
     state ranking from the four initial laws. Reporting the zero threshold separately
16                                                                                              16
     keeps the decision statement distinct from a claim of neural superiority.
17                                                                                              17

18                                                                                              18
           15. COMPLETE COMPILED-POLICY SERVICES AND ECONOMIC COMPARISONS
19                                                                                              19
       The new study separates three questions: the cost of representing a fixed NBO
20                                                                                              20
     policy, the complete cost of constructing and certifying a policy, and the actual cost
21                                                                                              21
     of using one controller rather than another. A representation benchmark cannot by
22                                                                                              22
     itself answer either of the latter questions. Conversely, a conventional comparator
23                                                                                              23
     receives the same innovation law, feasibility conditions and all-state comparison
24                                                                                              24
     principle, not a deliberately inefficient implementation chosen to favor the neural
25                                                                                              25
     operator.
26                                                                                              26

27                                                                                              27
                     15.1. Design, stopping rules and conventional baselines
28                                                                                              28

29     The design was fixed before the complete execution. The principal catalogue uses         29

30   the original constrained two-state economy of Section 12, horizons T = 2, 3, prices        30

31   p = 1, 4, and N = M ∈ {4, 8, 16, 32, 64}. The three methods are the compiled cone          31

32   witness, uniform tensor FVI and error-driven nonuniform tensor FVI. There are              32
     58

1    three isolated process repetitions for each method and economic cell: 36 services and     1

2    180 rungs. Every rung starts from primitives and uses its own future continuation. 2
3    All rungs are executed, including those after a target crossing; first-target work        3

4    charges the complete prefix, including every prior failure.                               4

5      The conventional adaptive method forms an own-future pilot on a 5 × 5 grid,             5

6    uses its coordinatewise second differences to allocate nonuniform dyadic knots, and       6

7    certifies with the actual largest coordinate gaps. Its pilot, allocation and repeated     7

8    target evaluations are charged. The section on nonuniform fitted-value comparison         8

9    in the supplement specifies the fixed heuristic and proves the strengthened nearest- 9
10   node certificate on these unequal grids. It is a conventional adaptive-coordinate FVI     10

11   comparator, not a sparse-grid method or an unrestricted optimal adaptive partition. 11
12     All methods share outward arithmetic, feasible action fractions and midpoint            12

13   integration with its deterministic remainder under the continuous uniform law. 13
14   Each service includes a finite acquired-state deployment checksum and durable             14

15   checkpoint output. Complete prefix wall and CPU times, method-specific eval- 15
16   uation counts, coefficient bit lengths, stored actor scalars, checkpoint bytes and        16

17   peak resident memory are recorded. The internal clock excludes startup and the            17

18   common non-economic warm-up; a separate process clock includes them. No inde- 18
19   pendent cost comparison is hidden inside that boundary: its joint service is reported     19

20   separately below.                                                                         20

21     Against uniform FVI, compiled witness is earlier in 3 of 30 common successful           21

22   target/repetition comparisons, later in 27, and tied in 0. Against error-driven FVI,      22

23   compiled witness is earlier in 15 of 30 common successful target/repetition compar- 23
24   isons, later in 15, and tied in 0. These are observed complete-prefix wall times, not a   24

25   hardware-general speed law. The comparison uses the prospective targets rather            25

26   than retrospectively selecting a favorable breakpoint. The full positive-tolerance par- 26
27   tition, including one-sided and joint nonattainment, is reported in the supplement        27

28   and machine-readable frontier. The error-driven rule returns uniform grids in all         28

29   210 recorded date models; all 60 adaptive checkpoints have exactly the same contin- 29
30   uations and actors as their uniform-FVI counterparts. Thus its additional observed        30

31   cost is pilot and allocation overhead, not evidence of a realized nonuniform-grid         31

32   improvement. The nonuniform verification theorem is proved and tested, but an             32

                   15.3. Direct constrained-policy decisions and information prices                           21

22     The direct comparison freezes the original N = 16 witness and FVI policies. It does                    22

23   not select the new R48 policies after examining their costs. Forty-eight policy-pair                     23

24   tasks cross the four economic cells, four initial laws and three observation contracts                   24

25   of Section 14. The full study contains 48 × 262144 = 12582912 paired paths. Nu- 25
26   merical path widths, every unresolved actor or sensor branch, endpoint moments,                          26

27   simultaneous intervals and decisions are deposited. Table XVI summarizes inter- 27
28   val hulls across the four declared initial laws; its rows are not all-state confidence                   28

29   bands. The supplement reports every interval separately. The zero threshold and the                      29

30   replacement-fee threshold 1/64 are different decision questions. A no-recoupment                         30

31   result is conditional on that predeclared theoretical fee. It neither establishes exact                  31

32   policy equality nor measures empirical welfare equivalence.                                              32

24   timum minimizes the uniform augmented upper account. Since the full-information                            24

25   certificate G is unchanged across observation precisions, the integer choice responds                      25

26   to the objective sensitivity and the price of information, not to an arbitrary selec- 26

20   against uniform and error-driven conventional FVI with all failed resolutions in- 20
21   cluded. Direct constrained costs and priced observation support explicit economic                         21

22   decisions for fixed policies under declared information contracts. These results                          22

23   extend the original program, rather than replace it with a different subject.                             23

24     The historical adverse findings remain: the original nonlinear optimizer catalogue,                     24

25   selected residual-only recertifications, unresolved neural–ridge signs, and mixed- 25
26   precision timing records are not reclassified. Identical native and ReLU envelopes                        26

27   remain one mathematical construction. The new deterministic repetitions do not                            27

28   estimate a population success probability for nonconvex optimization; the direct                          28

29   confidence statements concern their fixed policies under the sampling model; the                          29

30   stress clocks do not identify hardware-general scaling. The comparative claims                            30

31   in this article are restricted to the objects actually proved or executed, while the                      31

32   controlled-economy and application-specific theoretical scope remains intact.                             32
     64

1                       16. ECONOMIC APPLICATIONS AND CONCLUSION                               1

2                                                                                              2

3      The original applications concern policy-dependent recursive utility, endogenous        3

4    preference specifications, temporal selves, controlled diffusion problems, and dy- 4
5    namic games. Their complete economic statements and proofs are retained without           5

6    alteration. The centered and transport bounds explain how an NBO can support              6

7    economic counterfactuals when changes in future primitives are controlled. The pol- 7
8    icy theorem explains what must be verified for the actual policy. Neither statement       8

9    supplies an equilibrium or transversality condition absent from the application.          9

10     The nonlinear investment evidence adds an executable link from primitives to            10

11   learned continuations and directly certified policies. Its retained-policy diagnostic     11

12   demonstrates that a failed tight certificate can reflect the resolution of verification   12

13   rather than the quality of the deployed policy. Its direct coupled comparison answers     13

14   a different question—the relative cost of two policies—without confusing separate         14

15   regret bounds with a method contrast.                                                     15

16     The acquired-state result makes that returned policy implementable under a              16

17   stated information and arithmetic contract. The constrained economy supplies finite       17

18   covering, integration and repair routines; the direct scalar experiment supplies          18

19   simultaneous tolerance-based cost comparisons and a catalogue-adoption rule. 19
20   Their distinct assumptions and work accounts remain visible.                              20

21     The compiler adds a policy-preserving computational step to this program. It            21

22   connects the explicit neural continuation to an executable representation whose           22

23   per-query work no longer scans the full state cover. The economic implementation          23

24   adds two further choices: how precisely to acquire the state, and whether replacing       24

25   an installed controller pays for itself. The first is governed by a priced, certified in- 25
26   formation account; the second by direct expected costs with an explicit replacement       26

27   charge. They are not inferred by ranking regret bounds.                                   27

28     The empirical content is correspondingly separated. Frozen-object experiments           28

29   identify representation costs for the same policy; complete services compare fresh        29

30   own-future constructions against conventional FVI; direct intervals concern fixed         30

31   policies and declared initial laws. The higher-dimensional executions expose the          31

32   remaining tensor and innovation costs. They do not constitute a dimension-free            32
                                   NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                              65

1    approximation theorem, a calibration to welfare data, or a reliability law for unre- 1
2    stricted neural training. Within these boundaries, the operator supplies a construc- 2
3    tive route from economic primitives through a feasible implemented policy to a            3

4    verified resource decision.                                                               4

5      Neural Bellman Operators thus organize construction, certification, economic            5

6    reuse, and resource accounting around the policy actually returned. Their scientific      6

7    assessment requires all of these objects. Flexible conventional methods remain            7

8    important competitors; numerical activation is not a work saving, and a fixed             8

9    catalogue is not a general reliability theorem. Theorem 5 and Corollary 6 now supply      9

10   a separate deterministic reliability statement for an explicitly constructed neural       10

11   backend. They do not retroactively prove convergence of the earlier optimizers. 11
12   Preserving those distinctions makes the original economic program testable while          12

13   retaining its full theory and applications.                                               13

14                                                                                             14

15                                                                                             15
                     APPENDIX A: R EADING AND PRESERVATION MAP
16                                                                                             16

17     The main article is the authoritative R48 exposition of the existing Neural Bellman     17

18   Operators paper. It includes the controlled-economy formulation, centered transport       18

19   and policy comparison, constructive continuation and original witnesses, acquired- 19
20   state feasibility, exact compilation, and the economic decision analysis. The technical   20

21   supplement supplies the proofs and numerical accounts needed for those results,           21

22   as well as every new direct interval and the complete first-target time partitions. 22
23   All R47 main and supplement labels, including their inherited results, remain in the      23

24   corresponding active documents. The current response addresses the R47 report             24

25   item by item.                                                                             25

26     The experimental sections distinguish frozen historical evidence from new ex- 26
27   ecutions. Earlier optimizer, precision, scalar and coupled policy records are not         27

28   regenerated or relabeled. The full historical theory and application companions           28

29   are retained as ordinary files in the revision tree; preservation is not asserted to      29

30   be a new validation of every application. The main finite-horizon certificate needs       30

31   the monotonicity, cash-invariance, feasibility and comparison assumptions stated          31

32   here. The direct sampling result additionally uses conditional expectation and its        32
     66

1    sampling model. Controlled diffusions, infinite horizons, recursive utility domains      1

2    and equilibrium applications retain their separate analytic conditions.                  2

3      The source tree contains the ordinary manuscript, proofs, bibliography, table          3

4    generator, scientific sources, complete frozen records and build instructions. A clean   4

5    rebuild audits the records and regenerates the documents without a new scientific        5

6    run or an expiring artifact download. The publication audit binds the review anchor,     6

7    scientific source, evidence, tests and compiled documents. These checks establish        7

8    a reproducible submitted object; they are not a substitute for mathematical peer         8

9    review.                                                                                  9

1                       APPENDIX: C URRENT R52 DEVELOPMENT                                     1

2                                                                                              2
                               APPENDIX B: I NTRODUCTION
3                                                                                              3

4      A continuation value is useful to an economist because it changes a decision. Its       4

5    prediction error is not the loss of the policy actually implemented. Nor is a smaller     5

6    upper bound on that loss evidence that one policy has lower cost than another. When       6

7    observation, action selection and verification are costly, the numerical procedure        7

8    and the economic decision have to be evaluated together.                                  8

9      We develop Neural Bellman Operators as a policy-evaluation and feasible- 9
10   improvement method. The economic object remains a controlled economy with                 10

11   policy-specific continuations. The operator constructs each date’s continuation us- 11
12   ing its own fitted future, returns a feasible policy, and accounts for its loss against   12

13   the Bellman optimum. A general signed policy sandwich permits arbitrary fitted            13

14   candidates. A constructive affine–ReLU backend additionally produces the candi- 14
15   date from primitive moduli, without assuming that a nonconvex training algorithm          15

16   eventually succeeds. Exact compilation retains the original action witness through        16

17   off-grid evaluation and state-dependent feasibility repair.                               17

18     The principal question in this revision is how that construction produces a cer- 18
19   tified economic answer with finite resources. State coverage, action search and           19

20   integration have different error coefficients. Tying them to one grid size conceals       20

21   the allocation problem. We derive a separated primitive budget and an optimal             21

22   continuous allocation for its leading work proxy, including lower resource bounds         22

23   and dyadic implementation. The empirical comparison then records the complete             23

24   positive-tolerance frontier of a fixed common resource ladder, not only an isolated       24

25   favorable target. All failed earlier attempts belong to the work required to return a     25

26   policy.                                                                                   26

27     The next question is what the returned policy does. We evaluate the actual policies     27

28   at the declared first crossings, even when two generators cross at different reso- 28
29   lutions. Independent interval simulation preserves the original continuous initial        29

30   and innovation laws and encloses discontinuous actors and robust capacity repair. 30
31   Conditioning on construction information permits simultaneous cost inference after        31

32   this selection. The result does not infer a policy ranking from two certificates and      32
     68

1    does not extrapolate three deterministic timing repetitions into an optimizer-success    1

2    probability.                                                                             2

3      Two economic decisions make this distinction operational. First, a controller          3

4    replacement is worthwhile only if its actual gain exceeds the replacement fee. We        4

5    identify the directional gain intervals and the full break-even fee account, retaining   5

6    but no longer relying on a single generous charge. Second, observation precision is      6

7    chosen on the same finite bit grid on which the implemented policies are evaluated. 7
8    A simultaneous upper-net-cost selector has a regret bound relative to the best           8

9    precision in that catalogue. The bound holds for every nonnegative information           9

10   price on one coverage event. This is a result about actual net policy cost, different    10

11   from minimizing a conservative all-state loss account.                                   11

12     These advances do not depend on calling a classical envelope neural. A native          12

13   min-plus envelope and its exactly equivalent ReLU circuit are one method with            13

14   two encodings. The original-owner compiler is available to both. We compare that         14

15   method with uniform multilinear fitted-value iteration (FVI) and two separately          15

16   recorded conventional refinement rules. The original curvature-bisection block           16

17   remained uniform; the later graded block was nonuniform. A new residual-driven           17

18   comparator below records its realized geometry and all pilot work. Each generates        18

19   its own future labels and receives the same feasible action menu, continuous-law         19

20   integration and one-sided economic criterion. The evidence must determine the cost       20

21   of the returned policy as well as certificate sharpness. No uniform neural dominance     21

22   is built into the design.                                                                22

23                                                                                            23

24        B.0.0.1. Related methods. Lipschitz extension supplies the cone construction (Mc- 24
25   Shane, 1934), and separable distance transforms supply its linear-work nodal engine      25

26   (Felzenszwalb and Huttenlocher, 2012). Approximation in min-plus spaces has an           26

27   established dynamic-programming literature: Lakshminarayanan and Bhatnagar               27

28   (2014) study min-plus projected Bellman equations and supremum-norm errors. 28
29   Bellman-inequality methods likewise construct lower value bounds and policies;           29

30   Wang et al. (2015) use iterated inequalities with linear or semidefinite optimization. 30
31   Our argument does not claim priority for these principles. Its subject is their con- 31
32   nection to an own-future feasible policy, original-owner continuous compilation,         32
                                        NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                    69

1    acquired-state implementation, complete resource allocation and direct economic                      1

2    comparison of the returned objects. The empirical-Bernstein component uses the                       2

3    bounded-sample inequality of Maurer and Pontil (2009); conditional selection and                     3

4    numerical path enclosures specify the estimand to which it is applied.                               4

5      The broader NBO program includes controlled diffusions, recursive utility, en- 5
6    dogenous preferences, temporal selves and games. Those results and their as- 6
7    sumptions are retained in the complete development edition and its full proof                        7

8    supplement, together with the original training, matrix-factor and precision experi- 8
9    ments. The present article supplies a self-contained constructive theorem–evidence                   9

10   chain. Its direct-cost statistical theorems concern expected discounted costs, not an                10

11   undeclared extension to every nonlinear recursive application. This organization                     11

12   preserves the paper’s economic subject while making the current contribution and                     12

13   the actual comparison burden explicit.                                                               13

14     The additional improvement result addresses the economically important case                        14

15   where the certified construction returns a more costly policy than a conventional                    15

16   competitor. We preserve that incumbent and permit only actions whose actual                          16

17   reference advantage is certified nonpositive after the numerical and observation                     17

18   allowances. At the final date of the original investment model, exact continuous- 18
19   law integration makes this gate executable without assuming a successful policy- 19
20   value fit. Both generators receive the same repair. The resulting experiment isolates                20

21   removable final-action loss from the policy gap that remains; it does not rename the                 21

22   compiler as a uniquely neural advantage.                                                             22

23                                                                                                        23

24        APPENDIX C: T HE ECONOMIC OPERATOR AND ITS POLICY ACCOUNT                                       24

25     Consider dates t = 0, . . . , T and an invariant compact state domain X ⊂ Rd . At                  25

26   date t, the action belongs to a nonempty compact set At ( x ). The transition is                     26

27   Ft ( x, a, Zt+1 ), the current cost is ct ( x, a), and the terminal cost is g( x ). Write B0 = 1     27

28   and Bt = ∏ j<t β j , with β j > 0. An implemented policy includes its observation rule,              28

29   action selection and feasible numerical realization, not just its stored parameters.                 29

30     For a continuation v, define                                                                       30

31                                                                                                        31
        Qt (v; x, a) = ct ( x, a) + β t ρt (v( Ft ( x, a, Z ))),   Tt v( x ) = inf Qt (v; x, a).   (65)
32                                                                           a∈ At ( x )                  32
     70

1    Throughout the deterministic comparison, the evaluations ρt are monotone and                 1

2    cash invariant on a common domain containing all functions and constant translates           2

3    used below. They are therefore nonexpansive in the supremum norm. Continuous                 3

4    objectives, effective compact feasible correspondences and measurable minimizers,            4

5    or measurable arbitrarily near-minimizers with their error charged, are assumed. 5
6    The stipulated conditional recursion gives Bellman comparison against all ad- 6
7    missible adapted controls. In the experiments ρt is conditional expectation, costs           7

8    are bounded and innovations have their original continuous uniform law. The                  8

9    direct-cost inference in Section J is specific to expectation; it is not automatically an    9

10   inference theorem for nonlinear recursive utility or games.                                  10

11     A Neural Bellman Operator is a construction and evaluation procedure for (65). 11
12   It forms targets using its own fitted f t+1 , returns a neural continuation and a feasible   12

13   policy, and records an economic error allowance. It does not receive the optimal             13

14   continuation as a training label. Arbitrary trained networks can be evaluated by the         14

15   first result below. The subsequent constructive backend specifies how a network              15

16   and its actor are obtained without assuming successful nonconvex optimization.               16

       The Lipschitz-extension principle is classical (McShane, 1934). A native min-plus
11                                                                                                            11

12                                                                                                            12

13                                                                                                            13

14                                                                                                            14

       A policy certificate answers a safety question relative to the optimum. It need
20                                                                                                  20
     not answer the comparison with an installed controller. We now make actual cost
21                                                                                                  21
     reduction a constraint on the improvement step. The continuation representation,
22                                                                                                  22
     including a neural realization, supplies proposals; a centered, policy-specific evalua-
23                                                                                                  23
     tion determines whether to implement them. The incumbent is always an admissible
24                                                                                                  24
     fallback.

10   the two actions coincide, so the advantage is exactly zero, not β t wt+1 . Backward                         10

11   monotone comparison, starting from a common terminal cost, gives the first asser- 11
12   tion. Under expectation subtract Jtπ ( Xt ) along the µ trajectory and telescope the                        12

13   conditional expectations. The resulting identity is the expected discounted sum of                          13

14   true reference advantages, each bounded above by the accepted negative margin or                            14

15   by zero.                                                                                         Q.E.D.     15

16                                                                                                               16

17     The improvement principle is related to approximate policy iteration with safe- 17
18   guarded updates, including Metelli et al. (2021). We do not claim that monotone                             18

19   policy improvement itself is new. Here the relevant connection is between a signed                          19

20   own-policy evaluation account, action-centered numerical errors, original-owner                             20

21   construction, and the actual information-constrained action. The test is available                          21

22   to neural and conventional proposals alike. An exact native encoding of a ReLU                              22

23   continuation does not become a second independent method.                                                   23

24                                                                                                               24

25                                                                                                               25

17        C OROLLARY 26—Acquired-cell final improvement: In the declared investment                      17

18   economies, final conditional cost H ( x, a) = c( x, a) + βEg( F ( x, a, Z )) is strictly convex     18

19   in a, with second derivative at least 2p. Fix an observation cell C and the implemented             19

20   incumbent action aC . Choose a lattice minimizer aC∗ at the reported midpoint on the cell-wide      20

21   feasible set. Accept it only if an outward enclosure of H ( x, aC∗ ) − H ( x, aC ) is nonpositive   21

22   for all x ∈ C. Keep the incumbent on every other cell and at all earlier dates. The resulting       22

23   acquired policy weakly improves actual expected discounted cost for every initial state. This       23

24   guarantee does not compare it to a different full-information incumbent.                            24

25                                                                                                       25

26     The lattice is {k/4096 : k ∈ N0 }, truncated by the minimum capacity on the eight- 26
27   bit cell. Convex derivative bisection identifies the two neighboring lattice actions;               27

28   their actual cost contrast selects the midpoint proposal. The gate compares its cost                28

29   with the incumbent on the entire cell, not just at the midpoint. Exact rational point               29

30   comparison resolves a numerically ambiguous proposal tie. An uncertain true-state                   30

31   box may intersect several cells; the simulator retains their action hulls rather than               31

32   selecting a favorable cell.                                                                         32
                                      NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                             79

1      For each generator, old and repaired policies share their earlier trajectory. Their                       1

2    discounted cost difference therefore equals the conditional final contrast. Centering                       2

3    cancels state-only costs and tightens its interval enclosure. When two generators are                       3

4    compared, their trajectories differ and are simulated separately using common inno- 4
5    vations. Both comparisons concern actual policies; neither is obtained by subtracting                       5

6    two policy-loss certificates.                                                                               6

       The preceding improvement result compares an implemented policy with its

     own incumbent. We now connect that comparison to the original Bellman optimum.
11                                                                                                               11
     This distinction matters economically: a policy can improve strictly while remaining
12                                                                                                               12
     more costly than a conventional alternative. The result below preserves the original
13                                                                                                               13
     objective and gives explicit sufficient conditions for closing its optimality gap; it
14                                                                                                               14

31     The hypotheses separate three computational tasks. A trained continuation must                          31

32   be evaluated relative to the policy actually retained; the action search must ap- 32
                                     NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                    81

1    proximate the original feasible infimum; and the comparison must be valid over                    1

2    the available information set. A guarantee for only one of these tasks does not                   2

3    imply the theorem. The same conclusion applies to native and neural representa- 3
4    tions when they implement the same objects. In particular, it does not turn their                 4

5    common compilation saving into a neural-specific advantage. Safe policy improve- 5
6    ment is an established principle; the result here supplies the signed-width and                   6

7    acquisition-aware error account for the incumbent-preserving NBO construction.                    7

         The directed-cover assumption is substantive when constraints move with the
18                                                                                                     18
     state. It is not implied by a dense grid at the representative alone. A failed cover or
19                                                                                                     19
     a cell that cannot certify a comparison retains its incumbent; it must not be counted
20                                                                                                     20

22       P ROPOSITION 29—Sharpness of the evaluation-width coefficient: Under the theo- 22
23   rem’s assumptions, the coefficient two on β t wtk+1 in (83) cannot be uniformly replaced by a     23

24   smaller constant, even for a one-period deterministic problem with two actions, an exact          24

25   greedy proposal, and exact contrast arithmetic.                                                   25

26                                                                                                     26

27       The theorem is constructive but conditional on verified error budgets. It does not            27

28   assert that a continuous neural fit uniformly approximates a discontinuous acquired- 28
29   policy value, or that refining a fixed acquisition contract eliminates its information            29

30   floor. The executed investment study below certifies a final-date improvement. It                 30

31   is not an execution of T full improvement passes, nor numerical evidence of the                   31

32   finite-sweep near-optimality conclusion. Exact finite-state regression tests exercise             32

4                APPENDIX G: P OLICY- RELEVANT CONTINUATION ERRORS                                          4

5      A continuation error matters for an investment decision through the actions it                       5

6    changes. The signed-width account above removes a datewise level error. It can nev- 6
7    ertheless charge for a nonconstant error that has the same conditional expectation                     7

8    under every feasible action. We now obtain a sharper account without changing the                      8

9    Bellman optimum, the acquisition contract, or the incumbent-preserving rule.                           9
